- **commandments-csharp-absence**: Modelling a value that might not be there in C# — a return typed `T?`, a `return null` for "not found", an `is null` check or a `?? default` at a call site, a `FirstOrDefault()`, a `TryGetValue`, the null-forgiving `!`, or deciding between throwing, returning an empty collection and returning `null`. - **commandments-csharp-behaviour-per-method**: A C# parameter that picks which behaviour runs instead of feeding one. - **commandments-csharp-documentation**: How to document C#, and mostly not to. A `/// &lt;summary&gt;` is a line or two about the code as it is NOW; a `//` comment is rare and only explains a non-obvious *why*; never narrate the past or a change (\"previously…\", \"used to…\", \"refactored to…\"). - **commandments-csharp-duplication**: Copying a method body from one C# class into another — the same `Map`/`Validate`/`Format` written a second time under another name, or a near-copy that differs only in the key, the path or the message it uses. Read this BEFORE pasting a method you already wrote in another class, and when a duplicate C# function finding points here. - **commandments-csharp-enums**: Modelling a value that can only be one of a handful in C# — a status, a kind, a mode held as a `string` and compared (`status == "paid"`), a set of `const string` fields standing in for cases, or a `switch` over string literals repeated in several classes. - **commandments-csharp-fix-at-the-source**: Writing a C# constructor that calls a method on something it was handed, a `static` field that methods write to, or a fix for a C# finding that is tempting to patch where it showed up. - **commandments-php-guard-clauses-and-flow**: How a method body is shaped — validate preconditions at the TOP with early return/throw, keep the body flat (no if/elseif/else ladders, no deep nesting), and run the happy path last. - **commandments-python-behaviour-per-method**: A Python parameter that selects WHICH behaviour runs rather than feeding one. When a function's whole body is `if flag: … else: …`, it is two functions sharing a name, and every call reads `render(order, True)`. Read this before adding a `bool` parameter, before widening a required parameter to `X | None = None` so that leaving it out means 'all of them', and when a call passes a bare `True`/`False`. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/behaviour-per-method) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-class-layout**: Adding a class attribute, a constant, or a dataclass field to a Python class — especially one that already has methods — or placing a nested class, a `ClassVar` or an annotated field. Read this BEFORE you declare state below a `def`, and when a class-layout finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/class-layout) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-dependency-direction**: Adding an import between two of the project's own Python packages. Read this before `from shop.ui.shared import …` inside `shop.ui.elements`, before an import inside a function added to dodge a circular import, and when a namespace-cycle or namespace-dependency finding points here. A declared layer may only import the layers it said it may use. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/dependency-direction) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-documentation**: How to document Python, and mostly not to. A docstring is a line or two about the code as it is NOW; a `#` comment is rare and only explains a non-obvious *why*; never narrate the past or a change (\"previously…\", \"used to…\", \"refactored to…\"). Read this the moment you are about to write a docstring, a `#` comment, or an `Args:`/`Returns:` section. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/documentation) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-duplication**: Copying a function or method body from one Python module into another — the same `load`/`render`/`validate` written a second time under another name, or a near-copy that differs only in the path, the key or the message it uses. Read this BEFORE pasting a `def` you already wrote somewhere else, and when a `duplicate-python-function` finding points here. The fix is one function both call, parameterised by whatever actually differs. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/duplication) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-enums**: A fixed set of values in Python — statuses, kinds, modes — written as string literals compared at call sites (`if status == "paid"`, `kind in ("box", "pallet")`), as module constants, or dispatched on with a `match` over strings. Read this BEFORE comparing a value against a literal it is one of a handful of. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/enums) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-exceptions**: Writing a `raise`, a `try`/`except`, or an exception class in Python — or deciding what a function does when something goes wrong. Read this BEFORE you write `except Exception: pass`, an `except` that returns `None`/`[]`/`False`, `raise ValueError("…")` with a message built at the raise, or a `raise` inside an `except` without `from`. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/exceptions) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-fix-at-the-source**: Writing an `__init__` that calls out to something it was handed, a module-level or class-level variable that functions write to, or a fix for a Python finding that is tempting to patch where it surfaced. Read this BEFORE making a constructor do work, keeping state in a `global` or a class attribute, or adding a check at a call site, and when a `python-constructor-side-effect` or `python-mutable-static-state` finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/fix-at-the-source) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-flow**: Shaping a Python function body — a precondition or `None` check at the start of a `def`, an `if` that decides whether the rest of the function runs, an `if`/`elif`/`else` chain, a loop whose whole body sits under one `if`, a block nested three deep, or an `else:` after a branch that already returned or raised. Read this BEFORE writing any of them, and when a Python flow finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/flow) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-method-mood**: Naming a Python method or a `@property` — especially one that returns a `bool`, or one that changes the object and returns nothing. Read this BEFORE you name a method `hides`, `binds` or `spins`, and when a method-mood finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/method-mood) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-pass-the-object**: Writing a Python function that takes an object and an id and whose first move is to look one up in the other — `def rename(workflow, node_id)` doing `workflow.graph.node(node_id)` — or one that takes a value its caller had to convert, derive or compute a bool from first. Read this before adding an `_id` parameter beside the object it keys into, and when a pass-the-object finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/pass-the-object) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-repeated-call-helper**: When you write the same thing the same way at site after site in Python — the same keyword call (`replace(order, status=...)`, `model.copy(update={...})`), the same compound `if` condition, the same `isinstance(x, A) and isinstance(x.y, B)` narrowing. Read this before copying a condition or a keyword call from one function into another, and when a repeated-guard or repeated-call finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/repeated-call-helper) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-role-vocabulary**: Writing a Python class that holds a dict of things by key and hands them out, one that collects things to ask whether it holds one, or a chain of `if`s that picks the first handler that matches — or naming a class `*Registry`, `*Set` or `*Resolver`. Read this before you write `def get(self, key) -&gt; X | None` on a store, and when a role-vocabulary finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/role-vocabulary) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-tell-dont-ask**: Behaviour belongs with the data it works on. Read this BEFORE you write a Python function that loops over another object's collection or walks its parts to work out something that object could answer, and before an `if isinstance(x, A): … elif isinstance(x, B): …` ladder that asks what a value IS to decide what to do with it — the answer is a method on the type (`order.total()`, `shape.area()`). ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/tell-dont-ask) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-templates**: Writing a multi-line string a Python program emits — generated code, a config block, a report, a message. Read this the moment you find yourself building a list of lines and `"\n".join(...)`-ing it, so the OUTPUT stays readable in the source that produces it. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/templates) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-type-honesty**: Annotating an attribute or a parameter `X | None` that the design always has set, reading it back with `x.y if x else …` or `getattr(x, "y", default)`, filling a required `str` field with `""` to satisfy a signature, saving `self.x` to a local and restoring it later, or a `@property` that returns a constant. Read this BEFORE you widen a type to make something pass, and when a type-honesty finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/type-honesty) · ★1)
- **commandments-python-value-objects**: Passing or returning a `dict` whose string keys are a fixed record (`{"sku": …, "quantity": …}`), reading `row["field"]` or `payload.get("field")` on data your own code built, or adding a third parameter that always travels with two others. Read this BEFORE you shape data as a dict or grow a signature — the answer is usually a frozen dataclass. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/python/value-objects) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-absence**: Writing a fallback for a value that might be missing in any language (`??`, `or`, `.get(k, default)`) and then comparing the result, or looping over `items ?? []` / `items or []`. Read this BEFORE writing either, and when an absence finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/absence) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-arguments**: Read before adding a parameter, passing pieces of a value, or writing a result through an argument. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/arguments) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-dependencies**: Read before importing anything between modules or layers. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/dependencies) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-documentation**: Read before writing a comment or changing a declaration's documentation. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/documentation) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-duplication**: Read before copying a function, a compound condition, or a path to the same folder. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/duplication) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-enums**: Read before repeating a declared set of cases in a switch, membership check, or chain of comparisons. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/enums) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-exceptions**: Writing a `throw`/`raise`, a `try`/`catch`/`except`, or an exception class in any language. Read this BEFORE an empty catch, a catch that returns null or rethrows what it caught, a new failure thrown inside a catch, or `throw new Exception("…")`, and when an exceptions finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/exceptions) · ★1)
- **commandments-shared-flow**: Shaping a function body in any language: an `if` that decides whether the rest runs, an `else` after a branch that already returned, a loop whose whole body sits under one `if`, an if/else-if chain over one value, a ternary inside a ternary, a ternary or `&&` used as a statement, a statement every branch repeats, code after a `return`, or a parameter given a new value. Read this BEFORE writing any of them, and when a flow finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/shared/flow) · ★1)
- **commandments-typescript-absence**: Modelling a value that might not be there in TypeScript — a `?? default`, an `?.` chain, a `field?: T`, a `=== null` or `=== undefined` test, or deciding between `field?: T`, `T | null` and `T | undefined` on an interface. Read this BEFORE writing any of them in a .ts module or a component's script. TypeScript has TWO ways to be missing where PHP has one, and a type that admits absence the design never has is a lie the compiler will not catch. It is about the absence YOUR types declare, not about silencing the compiler on a DOM or library call that genuinely returns null. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/typescript/absence) · ★1)
- **commandments-typescript-duplication**: Copying a function body from one component or module into another — the same `load`/`format`/`submit` written a second time in a different `&lt;script setup&gt;` or `.ts` file, or a near-copy that differs only in the endpoint, the field or the label it uses. Read this BEFORE pasting a function you already wrote somewhere else, and when a `duplicate-function` finding points here. The fix is a shared function or composable that both call, parameterised by whatever actually differs. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/typescript/duplication) · ★1)
- **commandments-typescript-flow**: Shaping a function body in a `.ts` file or a `&lt;script setup&gt;`: a check that decides whether the rest of it runs, an `if`/`else if` chain, a block nested three deep, an `else` after a branch that already returned or threw, or a loop that pushes to, splices out of or deletes from the very collection it walks. Read this BEFORE writing any of them, and when a TypeScript flow finding points here. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/typescript/flow) · ★1)
- **commandments-vue-mirrored-server-type**: You are about to hand-write a TypeScript `interface`/`type` whose fields match a backend `Data` class — a `UserData`, an `OrderData`, the shape an endpoint returns. Read this BEFORE typing out fields that already exist on the server. If the backend owns the shape, the frontend must GENERATE its type from it, not re-declare it. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/vue/mirrored-server-type) · ★1)
- **commandments-vue-server-calls**: You are about to call `fetch(` in a component, write a server path like `'/plans/' + id` or `` `/api/tasks/${id}` ``, or set request headers by hand. Read this BEFORE you do: the server already declares its routes, and the frontend reaches them through the generated route helpers and one client module. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/vue/server-calls) · ★1)
- **commandments-vue-vue-components**: Extract a component when template markup REPEATS identically, or when an element in a large template reaches DEEP into nested data (data.user.firstName). Repeated markup is one component waiting to be born; a deep reach is a child that knows too much about the data shape and wants the mid-object as a prop. A dispatch (`&lt;SwitchCase&gt;` or a `v-if` chain on one subject) whose cases each render a whole view inline is a component doing a job per case, and a template past the project's declared element budget is one doing several. Read this BEFORE copy-pasting a block of template, reaching `a.b.c` i… ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/vue/vue-components) · ★1)
- **commandments-vue-vue-control-flow**: Dispatch on a single value with the published &lt;SwitchCase :value&gt; component (a slot per case), never a v-if / v-else-if chain that re-tests the SAME subject against a different literal. A chain of `x === 'a'` / `x === 'b'` is one decision wearing many conditionals. Read this BEFORE writing a v-if/v-else-if chain in a Vue template. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/commandments/vue/vue-control-flow) · ★1)
- **commandments-writing-detectors**: How to write a commandment of your OWN — a project-local rule that judges every file from then on. Read this BEFORE writing or changing a rule in `.commandments/custom/`, when the user asks for a new rule/check/detector, when `commandments make` points you here, or when you are about to reach for a regex to inspect code. ([jessegall/code-commandments](https://github.com/jessegall/code-commandments/tree/HEAD/skills/writing-detectors) · ★1)
- **commando-devices**: Take, wait for, claim and release Commando device locks on shared physical phones, the Android emulator and heavy Mac builds or exports. Use before touching a plugged-in device, booting or reconfiguring the shared emulator, profiling, or running xcodebuild / expo export / pod install / gradlew builds that a lock covers, and in scripts that do any of these. ([laeijebor/commando](https://github.com/laeijebor/commando/tree/HEAD/skills/commando-devices))
- **commando-prs**: Link pull requests to their originating Commando pane. Use when creating a PR or editing its description from a tmux pane, including gh pr create and GitHub MCP tools. ([laeijebor/commando](https://github.com/laeijebor/commando/tree/HEAD/skills/commando-prs))
